and include `lastDetail.claims.length` + status in the detail. And use waitFor on report-md with a shorter timeout, then check. Let me restructure:

<details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

**Found it — the real bug.**

`liveChannels(state, limit = 4)` takes the **last 4 channels in insertion order** for the current stage. During the verdict stage there are 8 concurrent channels (`verdict:C1`..`verdict:C8`? here 5 claims + maybe rerun). With limit 4, only the newest 4 are shown. But that's not the reasoning issue either.

Wait, the real issue: during the **answer** stage there may be 10 probes → channels answer:P1..P10 (only up to MAX_PROBES... probes run in rounds, 2 per turn? Actually probe cap per round 2, so answer channels: 2 per round → up to 2-3 channels per stage). Hmm.

Actually here's the thing: `liveChannels` returns `.slice(Math.max(0, channels.length - limit))` — the LAST `limit` channels by `Object.keys(state.streams)` order = insertion order. Since verdict has 5 (or 8 in live) channels, the last 4 are shown. Fine — each of those has reasoning (verdict cassettes all have reasoning) → `live-reasoning` should appear for those 4 rows.

So why not seen?! Unless the verdict stage's channels are all equal to... Hmm.

Wait — maybe the run in the browser **never got to the answer/verdict stages with reasoning** because the driver's C section clicks run and the *first* session after reload ran fine (claims 5 ✓ status done ✓) → verdicts happened → reasoning streamed.

Hold on: is `reasoning` actually delivered through the SSE `stream` event? Let me check `pipeline.ts`'s sink: it forwards `{channel, kind, text}`. In `http/app.ts`, the run route wraps with a sink that writes `event: 'stream'` with the chunk JSON. The web's `parseSseBlock`/`asStreamChunk` maps the SSE `stream` event to `StreamChunk`. Let me check `streamRun` in api.ts: does it handle `event: 'stream'` and pass `kind` through? Maybe it maps data → chunk but drops `kind` (e.g., builds `{channel, stage, kind: ..., text}`) — need to verify. Actually state.ts expects `chunk.kind` and `chunk.text`, and it works for text, so kind is "text" at least. If the SSE handler maps `data.kind` correctly, reasoning would work.

Let me check `parseSseBlock` / `asStreamChunk` in api.ts around 1010-1090.

Another possibility: the *server* never sends reasoning because `withStreaming` is applied to the client in `pipeline.ts` only when `input.sink` is provided, and `run.ts` uses `stageRequest`... fine.

Let me check the replay client's stream: maybe it yields reasoning only when `entry.response.reasoning` is set AND `entry.response.chunks` is absent. Look at replay.ts lines 30-80.

Let me read both api.ts parseSseBlock/asStreamChunk and replay.ts stream.

The plumbing is fine. So why was `[data-shot=live-reasoning]` never captured in the DOM?

Key insight candidate: `liveChannels` returns the last 4 channels **in insertion order of `state.streams` keys**. During the verdict stage, channels are created in arrival order. Each row shows `thought` if reasoning non-empty. So at least some row should show reasoning... 

UNLESS the reasoning chunks arrive for channels that are NOT in the last 4 and the displayed 4 are the ones *without* reasoning — no, all verdict cassettes have reasoning.

Hmm wait — maybe during the run, `state.phase === 'running'` but `state.activeStage` — `liveChannels` requires `state.activeStage !== null`. Fine.

Alternatively: **the reasoning chunks may never be emitted by the replay client** because `entry.response.chunks` exists (recorded from the scripted client's stream, which chunks reasoning first then text) and the replay path may use `chunks` when present — replay.ts: "先 reasoning 分片（entry.response.reasoning 非空时）再 entry.response.chunks（有就用录制分片）最后 yield done". That yields reasoning ✓.

So the most likely explanation is a *timing/visibility* one: `thought` is rendered only for channels in `live`, and the DOM element exists only while that row is rendered. My poll reads `[data-shot=live-reasoning]` which is the FIRST matching element — fine, length > 0.

Hmm, but wait: `readableStreamText(buffer.reasoning)`: reasoning starts as '' then accumulates. First chunk may be e.g. "这" → returns "这" ✓ non-empty → rendered.

Unless... `tailText(readableStreamText(...), 220)` — if `readableStreamText` returns something like `\n` → `.trim()`? It returns `out.trim()` only in the JSON branch; in the raw branch it returns `text` unmodified. If the first reasoning chunk is `\n` or ` ` then thought could be whitespace — but it accumulates fast.

OK, empirical diagnosis wins over speculation: add to the poll a capture of the whole `[data-shot=live]` innerText at its max, plus a flag for whether '思考 + 输出' ever appeared, and if the flag never appeared, ALSO capture the raw state via the page? We can't read React state directly... but we CAN instrument: after the run, check the server-side SSE by making a second run request from **Node** with fetch and inspecting chunk kinds (kind === 'reasoning' presence). That's a strong check — but a second run needs a second session (or a fresh one) — Node can create one: POST /api/sessions {useDemo:true} then POST /run and read the SSE body, counting reasoning chunks. That verifies the server path independent of the DOM. 

That's a good, cheap diagnostic to run in the same ROUND: after the browser run, in Node: create a new session & stream (the server may reject concurrent... it's fine sequentially) and count `kind === 'reasoning'` occurrences. If Node sees reasoning chunks, the server is fine and the DOM issue is front-end (likely `live` rows or pacing). If Node sees none, the server/replay is the problem.

Hmm, but careful: creating and running an extra session via Node mutates the app state (adds a session) — the driver's later assertions count sessions (e.g., "新开一场 1 → 2", delete loop counting). An extra session would break those counts. I can delete it afterward (DELETE /api/sessions/:id) ✓.

Where to put it: in the C section, after the browser run completes: create Node session, run with SSE, count reasoning/text chunk kinds, delete it. Good.

Then, if Node sees reasoning chunks, the DOM issue is front-end and worth investigating: maybe the `live-reasoning` element appears only for channels whose text is empty... no.

Wait wait. I think I might have it: **`thought === ''` check uses `readableStreamText(buffer.reasoning)`, but `readableStreamText` is applied to `buffer.reasoning`, and for the *answer* stage the reasoning is Chinese prose ✓.** Hmm.

Another candidate: the driver's poll calls `textOf('[data-shot=live-reasoning]')` — `textOf` returns `(document.querySelector(...) || {}).innerText || ''`. For a `<p>` with `whitespace-pre-wrap` and italic, innerText works ✓.

Hmm, one more: React may not re-render the live rows often enough? It re-renders on every state update ✓.

Let me consider the pacing: the driver's C section may set pace to slow *before* the run (there's a "播放节奏" control in B). If pace is slow, the event queue delivers events slowly (e.g., 250ms per event). The stream chunks are delivered via a separate queue at STREAM_TICK_MS. If the *stage* events lag behind, `activeStage` may be 'verdict' while... no, channels still match.

Slow = 220 ms per event. The slow reveal:
- Events (non-stream): each 220 ms. If there are ~70 events → 15 s.
- Stream chunks: 3 per 40 ms → 516 chunks → ~6.9 s.

But they're independent timers running concurrently. Total slow reveal ≈ max(15 s, 7 s) ≈ 15-20 s. Meanwhile the actual server run in replay mode takes <1 s. So the driver's poll loop (which stopped when the run button re-enabled at ~1 s) captured only ~5 events revealed → hence "maxLiveRows 1" and "no reasoning" and later the D section read a state that was ~5 events in.

So the fix: after clicking run, poll until the UI catches up (claims pane shows 已判定 5 条), which takes ~20-30 s. Then all assertions are on a caught-up UI. And the reasoning check: during the poll loop, the reveal walks the answer and verdict stages; the live pane will show channels with reasoning.

The live pane rows: `liveChannels(state)` — let me confirm what StageRail shows (lines 190-230) to make sure the check's selector strategy is right. Also, the reasoning chunks arrive in the streamQueue; the events for the 'answer' stage arrive in the other queue. Both paced. The stream chunks for answer/verdict channels will be applied over ~7 s total, while the stage events are spread over ~15 s. The live pane shows channels of the active stage. For the answer stage: active stage = 'answer' after its stage event is dispatched; then the stream chunks for the answer channels are already applied (they were applied early, all within the first 7 s) → the rows will exist with reasoning ✓, and the label will read '思考 + 输出' ✓ (since reasoning chunks were applied before the text chunks for the same channel — the queue preserves order within the stream queue; reasoning comes first in each channel's yielded order ✓).

Now 65/66 — only H fails: waiting for the compare pane to contain '变好' times out (90 s).

Why does the rerun fail? Let's think:
- H clicks `[data-shot=revision-rerun]`.
- That calls `handleRerun` → `startRun({resumeText: revised.text, ..., parentSessionId: sandbox.sessionId})`.
- The rerun creates a new session with the revised resume; then runs.
- `compare` is fetched after the SSE stream ends → the CompareBar shows the comparison (变好 4 条 etc.).

Possible failures:
1. The rerun never starts: `revision-rerun` is `disabled={props.busy}` — busy false ✓. But wait: H clicks it while... hmm, G just adopted; busy false ✓.
2. The rerun starts but FAILS (server error): e.g., the contract fingerprint mismatch → the run fails; then `/compare` still returns the comparison? In App, `setCompare(null)` at the start of startRun, and in the finally `fetchCompare(sessionId).then(setCompare)`. If the session was created and then the run failed, compare would still be fetched → it'd show the comparison (based on the newly created session with 0 claims? then `comparison === null` → "没有可比的判定"). The wait for '变好' would fail.
3. The rerun in **replay mode** requires cassettes for the revised resume's prompts — the offline demo now includes a recorded second run (149 cassettes) ✓ but that's only for the *demo seed* resume. Here the driver used the demo resume + adopted 5 revisions → the revised text = the demo's revised text? Wait: in the offline demo, the second run uses ALL 5 revisions adopted (the seed adopts C1..C5). The driver adopted *some* set (5 处 changed = how many candidates?). Hmm, G says '改成了 5 处' — the same as the seed's recorded rerun (the seed's rerun adopts all claims). So the revised text might match the cassette's recorded second run exactly ✓.

But the driver in B did: 点模板 (加载了医疗模板) then reload + 再点示例 (demo resume) — the panel/interviewers may differ from the seed's default panel! The seed's default panel = DEFAULT_PANEL (tech/hr/hiring_manager). If the driver's session panel is the same (after reload + use-demo, the panel state is default) ✓. But the B section toggled a role off and back on ✓.

Hmm, but actually the driver's first run worked in replay mode ✓ (5 claims, 5 verdicts), so the first run's cassettes matched. The rerun needs *different* cassettes (the second-run cassettes recorded by the seed). Those exist for the seed's exact revised text + panel + difficulty/style.

The driver's style/difficulty: 常规/常规 (reset to normal ✓ via style-normal / difficulty-normal). Hmm, but in B it clicked style-bold then style-normal ✓, pace-slow stays.

The revised text after the driver's adoption: the driver adopted '全部采纳' (5 处) → applyRevisions with all claims' suggestions + the supplement? Wait, D added a supplement ('测试补的事实' for C1). In G the driver clicked `revision-accept-all` (全部采纳) which includes the supplement insertion! The seed's recorded rerun adopted only the 5 suggestions (no supplement). So the driver's revised text has the extra supplement sentence inserted → the second-run prompts DIFFER from the recorded cassettes → **cassette-missing** → the rerun fails with a fatal error (回放模式找不到这一条记录).

That's the real cause. Is it a product bug? It's the designed behavior: in replay mode, any prompt not covered by the cassettes fails loudly (by design, D-something: 回放模式找不到卡带就抛错并提示用 record 模式跑一次). So the driver's H section is testing something the offline demo can't cover because of the extra supplement.

So the fix for the driver: in H, don't have the supplement in the revised text — i.e., reorder: do H's rerun BEFORE adding the supplement (D), or clear the supplement selection... Actually simplest: in D, add the supplement test, then **delete/undo** it before G/H? The driver's D section: '补一句真话能存下并给出结论'. Then G adopts all (including the supplement). Hmm.

Alternatively, H's rerun can be expected to *fail* in a specific way (which is itself a valuable check: 回放模式找不到卡带时会明确报错，而不是静默降级). But the failure message must be user-visible. Then H's assertions would change: assert that the rerun surfaces a clear error? That would mean the demo's "改完再跑一遍" doesn't work for the driver's exact scenario — which is a legitimate limitation to report to the user: **离线回放在你手动改了简历（或补了一句真话）之后再跑，会因为没有对应卡带而失败**。Hmm! But wait — in the *real* UI flow for a judge: 一键示例 → 跑一场 → 采纳改写 → 改完再跑一遍. The seed recorded the rerun for exactly the demo resume + all 5 suggestions adopted. If the judge clicks only PART of the suggestions (e.g., adopts 3 of 5), the revised text differs → cassette-missing → the rerun fails with an error!

That's a REAL usability gap for the offline demo: the "改完再跑一遍" only works if you adopt the exact same set the seed adopted. The UI does have revision checkboxes (the judge may adopt a subset). So a judge who adopts a subset and clicks 改完再跑一遍 gets an error message. Is the error message clear? In replay mode, `LlmError cassette-missing` with a message telling them to use record mode ✓ (it's explicit, not silent). But the UI: does the run error surface as a notice/hint? The run would emit a fatal error event → the UI shows an error. Hmm, that's "明确失败" per design (ADR: 缺卡带绝不静默降级).

Options:
(a) Accept it and document it (and make the e2e/driver assert the clear error).
(b) Make the demo seed record the rerun for every adoption subset — combinatorial explosion (2^5 = 32 variants) → not viable.
(c) Make the UI hint at it: in offline replay, when the judge clicks 改完再跑一遍 with a non-seed revised text, warn upfront. Hard to know.
(d) Better: make the *replay* fall back... no, that breaks the "无卡带必须显式失败" principle.

Hmm, what does the *shipped* product do for the common path? If the judge adopts ALL suggestions (the natural: 全部采纳 → 确认采纳), the rerun works ✓ (the seed recorded exactly that). Our driver adopted all *candidates* which included the extra supplement it added manually. So the demo works for the natural path ✓.

So: for the driver, the fix is to make H's scenario match the natural path: don't add the supplement before H, or remove it. Order in the driver: D (adds supplement) → ... → G (adopt all, including the supplement) → H (rerun).

Could we improve the demo coverage? Options:
- Record the rerun for "all suggestions adopted" (done) AND also for "suggestions + one supplement"? The supplement text is user-typed — infinite variety → impossible.
- Better: make the demo's 改完再跑一遍 robust by *not* relying on cassettes for arbitrary text... impossible in replay.
- Alternative: make the UI *aware*: in replay mode, when the judge clicks 改完再跑一遍 with a revised resume that has supplements (or any manual insertions), warn: 「离线回放只能重放预录过的改写版本（一键示例 + 全部采纳这 4 条建议）。你手动加过一句话，联网模式才能跑。」 That's a nice touch but requires knowing whether the revised text matches the seeded one — impossible generally.

Hmm, actually a simpler framing: the *error message* from the run already surfaces. Let me check what the UI shows when the run fails with cassette-missing: the error event (fatal) → the UI shows an error note. Our driver's L section showed 0 console errors and 0 failed requests in the successful round... but H's run failed — hmm, would that appear as a failed request? The run POST returned 200 with an SSE `event: fatal`, so no HTTP error. The UI should show the error text (a notice). H's section then timed out waiting for the compare pane to say '变好' — because compare was fetched for the failed session (which has no claims → comparison null → '没有可比的判定').

So the judge sees: a red error message + 「比上次/没有可比的判定」. Not a crash, but not great.

Improvement idea (cheap, and a real robustness win): when the rerun fails due to missing cassettes in replay mode, the CompareBar could explain. But better: at the *start* of the rerun in replay mode, we could check whether the revised text equals the round's... no.

Alternative improvement: in offline replay mode, disable the supplement insert? No — the supplement is a legit feature (A1) and its cassettes ARE recorded (the seed's first run has the supplement? no, the seed doesn't add supplements).

Honest assessment: this is an inherent limitation of an offline-recording demo; the product's live mode has no such limit. The right deliverables: (1) document it in the help/FAQ (「离线回放只能重放预录过的那几条路；你手动改过简历或补过一句话之后再跑，会明确告诉你缺卡带，联网模式下没有这个限制」) and (2) — the nicer fix — make the **rerun in replay mode fall back gracefully**? No, must fail loudly.

Hmm, hold on. There's a much better idea: the cassette lookup is by prompt digest. The rerun's prompts differ only in the resume text. What if the demo seed recorded the rerun for the *supplement* variant too? The supplement text is arbitrary... but the *judge's most likely* path is: 一键示例 → 跑 → 全部采纳 → 改完再跑一遍 ✓ (recorded). Adding a supplement is an extra feature they might try, then rerun.

Given the time budget (and that the offline demo is a bonus), I'll: fix the driver, report this limitation to the user with a concrete suggested improvement (and optionally implement a small UI hint in replay mode when a rerun fails with cassette-missing: "离线回放只重放了「一键示例 + 全部采纳」这一条路；你手动改过内容，联网模式才跑得动。").

Actually — I can implement a cheap, genuinely useful improvement: in the CompareBar/error path, when the session status is 'failed' and mode is replay, show an explanatory note. Let me check what the UI shows for a failed run. The driver's H failure suggests the UI showed something; let me capture the actual UI text on failure to know what the judge sees. I could add a diagnostic to the driver (H section) that prints the compare bar + any error note text when the wait times out.

Given effort/benefit, the best plan:
1. Fix the driver D section (delete the supplement) + add the delete assertions.
2. Rerun → expect 66/66.
3. Report to the user: all functions pass; plus the one real limitation found (offline replay + manual content edits → rerun fails loudly), with the recommendation.

Also: the seed records the rerun for the standard path; I could ALSO record a variant where the supplement is included — but the supplement text is the user's, so no.

Hmm, but wait. There's a *better fix* available for this specific rough edge, and it's cheap: make the demo's **rerun** not depend on the resume text at all... The rerun's *probe* stage sends the revised resume into the prompt → digest changes. Could the demo script's `respond` be keyed to stable markers instead? The cassette digest is computed from the prompt, so no.

OK, let me also reconsider: is it *really* acceptable that a judge clicking around (adopting 3 of 4 suggestions, say) hits an error? The demo experience is a scored criterion (用户体验). A judge is likely to click '全部采纳' (the default-ish) → works. And if they adopt a subset, they see an error saying the offline recording doesn't cover it. Hmm, that could look bad in a demo.

Improvement: make the **offline rerun** work for any revised text by recording a *coarser* variant: instead of relying on the exact resume, the seed could record the rerun with a "canonical" resume... no.

Alternative pragmatic improvement: in replay mode, when the rerun fails because a cassette is missing, the UI could offer: 「这一版在离线演示里没有录到卡带。点这里用**原始简历**再跑一遍，或者在投料台切到联网模式。」 Hmm, still a dead end.

Better pragmatic improvement (real feature): **在离线回放下，把「改完再跑一遍」做成对当前改写的确定性重放**：no.

I think the honest answer: document the limitation in help.ts FAQ ("离线回放重放的是预录的那一条路：你手动改过简历内容之后再跑，会明确报缺卡带；联网模式没有这个限制") and add it to the report to the user as the one substantive finding. Plus: it's arguably good that it fails loudly rather than silently producing fake results (that's the整 ADR principle: 缺卡带绝不静默降级).

Let me now patch the driver D section and rerun. Also check: after deleting the supplement, does the claim card still show it? The check '补一句真话能删掉' asserts the text is gone from the claims pane ✓.
